TABLE I.
AREA, HOUSES AND POPULATION.
1. This Table shows for each District and State the area, the number of
towns and villages, the number of occupied houses. and the total urban and
rutal population, male and female. The Districts are arranged, in this and

other Tables, in the order of Administrative

Divisions, with the only exception

that the figures of Delhi Province have not been shown in this Volume. a
separate Volume for that Province being prepared on the present occasion,

2. ** Houses*’ in colums 6 to 8 means census houses. For a discussion
of the definition of this term, see the closmg paragraphs of Chapter I of the
Report Volume. Briefly, the census house is the Saslne gince of the commensal
family ; but any non-residential building which contained a single individual

on the Census night was also reckoned as a“ Howse, *’
3.

The differences-between some

of the areas shown in this Table and

those given in the corresponding Table of 1921 are due to territorial transfers.
The recent Survey figures as communicated by the Department

of Survey of

India for Rohtak, Gujrat, Shahpur and Sheikhupura Districts have been entered

after adjustment for subsequent changes’; in the case of other Districts those sup-

plied by the Survey of India and reported in the Tables of Agricultural Statistics
of the Punjab for the year 1929-30 have been adopted without alteration.
4,

The territorial transfers that have occurred since the last Census are :—

(4) Inter-District transfers in the Ferozepore, Lahore, Sialkot, Sheikhupura, Montgomery, Lyallpur and Multan Districts.
(4) A transfer of one square mile from Ferozepore District to Bikaner
State.
(c) A transfer of two square miles from Kangra District to Mandi State.

(d) A transfer of less than one square mile from Bulandshahr District

(U. P.) to Gurgaon District and vice versa, caused by alluvion and
diluvion.

(e) A transfer of less than one square mile from Muzafiarnagar District
(U. P.) to Karnal District caused by alluvion and diluvion.
5. Inthe case of Indian States, the figures of 1921 have been repeated,
as figures from Survey Department were received too late for adoption.

6.

“ Biloch Trans-Frontier” is the hilly tract across the border

of the

Dera Ghazi Khan District proper. It is administered by Tumandars (Tribal
Chiefs) under the control of a Political Assistant to the Deputy Commissioner,
The tract has not yet been regularly surveyed and the area given in the last Census
Report has been repeated.
.

population statistics

for tahsils will befound in Provincial Tables I and If at the end of this
OMumé,

Note.—Tho figures in italics below the District figures relate to Cantonmenta.

TABLE

Il.

VARIATION IN POPULATION DURING THE LAST FIFTY YEARS.
:

l. In this Table the figures of the present census are compared with those
of previous censuses.

2. The territorial transfers (a) inter-District and (6) with the United Provinces have affected the population of the following Districts :—
Gurgaon,

Karnal,

Ferozepore,

Lahore,

Sialkot,

Sheikhupura,

Montgomery, Lyallpur and Multan.
3. ‘The population of areas transferred has been taken into account and
the figures of former censuses adjusted accordingly. The total population of the.
Province in 1881, 1891, 1901, 1911 and 1921 has been increased by 402, 412, 488,
474 and 454 persons respectively on account of transfer of area from the United

Provinces of Agra and Oudh.

TABLE IV.
TOWNS CLASSIFIED BY POPULATION WITH VARIATION FOR FIFTY YEARS.
1. For Census purposes

a town

has been defined to include every

Municipality, every Cantonment, all the Civil Lines not included
in municipal
limits, and every other continuous collection of houses inhabited by not less than

5,000 persons, which though not a Municipality possesses urban characteristics.
2. The question whether a colléction of residential houses should or

should not be classed under the last category was determined by

the Superin-

tendent of Census Operations in correspondence with local officers before the
Census with reference to the probable population and consequently in many

cases the limit of 5,000 was not reached.
with

more

than 5,000

characteristics.

inhabitants

were

On the other hand many places

omitted

as

not

had

they

The headquarters of all Punjab States, except the Simla

urban

Hill

States, have been included in this Table irrespective of their population.
3. No attempt has been made to adjust the figures affected by changes

in the area of towns during the last decade, and the figures given in the previous

Census

have been repeated.
4, The first five lines in the Table give the urban population of the
Punjab, and in regard to the past five Censuses they do not represent the
ageregate of the items that appear below them.

V.

TOWNS ARRANGED

TERRITORIALLY WITH POPULATION
BY RELIGION,
1. In the case of Municipalities with Cantonments or Civil Lines adjoining
them, the whole area has been treated as a single town but separate figures have
been given below the total in smaller type for the Municipalities, Cantonete.

ments,

2. Persons returned under the head “ Others’ have been classified and
thrown into religions as noted on the title page of Table XVI.
3. The abbreviations used in this Table are :—
M.=Mumnicipality. C. L.=Civil Lines.
Cantt.=Cantonment,
4. The figures of Aryas and Brahmos have been included in those of
5.

TABLE VI.
BIRTH-PLACE.
1, This Table is divided into four parts. Part A, the Provincial
Summary, contains statistics of birth-place for the Province as a whole, as well as

for British Territory and Punjab States. Part B gives the same information, for
each District and State. Part C gives statistics of birth-place by Districts and
States within the Province for the colony Districts of Gujranwala, Sheikhupura,
Shahpur, Montgomery, Lyallpur, Jhang and Multan, and Bahawalpur State.

Part D gives the information as in Part 6, for selected towns and cities,

2. Throughout this Table the phrase “ within British Domrtons,”
should be taken to mean, “ within the British Empire and Mandated Territories
except Iraq.”

TABLE VII.
AGE, SEX AND CIVIL CONDITION.
1. The age at this Census

was recorded

according to the nearest

birthday. At the last Census, age meant the number of years completed. Each
age-group includes the persons who have attained the ages represented by the
years constituting the group except the top year. For ex
example, the age-group
5-10 includes all persons

whose age is either

5, 6, 7, 8 or 9 years;

whose age is 10 go into the next higher group, 10-15.

2. This Table is divided into three parts.
~e-

-

—

,

i
:
2
ia"
-_

persons

Part A shows the distribution

for the Punjab as a whole of the total population and for each main religion by

age, sex and civil condition.

Figures for Ad-Dharmis
have been shown separately

for the first time. Part B shows the distribution for British Territory, for Punj
States (both groups), and for each District and State, of the total population
and
of the five principal religions (Hindu, Muslim, Christian, Jain and Sikh). The
age-groups in Part B are quinary, as in Part A, up to the ageof 20 and thereafter
decennia Fawn to60, the final group being 60 and over, Part C gives similar
details, with the addition of the races of * Others ” (other religions which include
‘Buddhist, Zoroastrian, Jew and Ad-Dharmi) for cities and selected towns to which
Lyallpur, Ludhiana and Sargodha have been added on the present occasion.

Note 1. ‘Tho age statisticn for placts treated as towns for Ceutus purposes
aregiven in Part I1]The age statiation for selected Tabsile bave not been tabulated.
Nole2, Ago statinticsfor Delhi Province appest ina separate Report,

TABLE VIII.
CIVIL CONDITION BY AGE FOR SELECTED CASTES AND TRIBES.
1. The castes selected for this Table are those which contributed at least
$ per mille to the total population of the Punjab in 1921, the crimmal tribes,

Bawaria, Harni, Pakhiwara and Sansi, being also included.
2. For the purposes of this Table, the Punjab has been split up into four
main divisions, viz :—

A.—Eastern Punjab, including the Ambala Division except Simla
District and the States of Loharu, Dujana, Pataudi, Kalsia and
Sirmoor.

B.—Central Punjab, Hills, including the Simla and Kangra Districts,
the Simla Hill States and the States of Bilaspur, Mandi, Suket
and Chamba.

O.—Central

Punjab, Plains, including the Jullundur Division except

the Gujrat, Lyallpur and Jhang
Kangra District, Lahore Division,
Districts, the Phulkian States, and the States of Kapurthala,

Faridkot and Maler Kotla.
D—Western

Punjab,

including

and Multan
Rawalpindi

3,

The figures

the

remaining

Districts

m_

the

Divisions and the State of Bahawalpur;

and these are referred to as A, B,

Cand D in column 2 of the Table.

for some localities and religions, not

shown in 1921

(‘Table LX), are now shown when so warranted by the numerical strength,
4. In this Table, figures for Arya (Vedic Dharm), Brahmo and Dev-

Dharm, are includedin those for Hindus, but to illustrate the civil condition
of those classes, separate figures have been printed as Table VILI-A in Part HI
of the Report.

TABLE XI.
OCCUPATION OF SELECTED CASTES, TRIBES OR RACES.
1. This Table shows the occupations followed by certain castes, as well
as Europeans, Armenians and Anglo-Indians.

2. The number of earners, following the traditional

occupation of the

caste
as their principal means of livelihood, 1s shown in columns 10 and 11, and

as a subsidiary means of livelihood in columns 12 and 13. The number of
earners, who have some other occupation as their principal means of livelihood, is
shown in column 18 and onwards.
3. The figures of all working dependants appear in columns 7 and 8, and
of those following their traditional occupations in.columns 14 and 15.
4. In the case of Europeans and Anglo-Indians, no occupation has been
assumed to be traditional.
5. The castes and the localities for this Table are the same as in Tables
VIII and XIV, with the exception that Europeans ard Anglo-Indians have been
shown for the whole of the Province with details of the Districts which returned
fifty or more persons of each nationality, The number of Armenians being very
small, their figures have been given for the whole Province.

TABLE XIII.
LITERACY BY RELIGION AND AGE.

__ 1. This Table is divided into three parts. Part A shows the literacy by
religion and age for the Province as a whole and detail for the British Territory
and Punjab States by main religions (Hindu, Jain, Sikh, Muslim and Christian),
Part B gives the figures for Districts and States by main religions, and Part C
details similar to B for cities and selected towns.

—

_ 2, Christians in the main Table include all races. The figures for Indian
Christians for the whole Province are given below, as also the detail by main sects.

TABLE XV.
LANGUAGES.
1. Part I, shows the mother tongues of the persons living in the Province.

If the mother tongue returned was a dialect it has been included im the language
to which it belonged, according to Sir George Grierson’s scheme of classification
contained in his Linguistic Survey of India.
2. Further detail of languages found in small numbers and lumped
together in the main Table appears below :—

PROVINCIAL
AND

AREA

TABLE

POPULATION

Il.

OF TAHS

1. The figures of area have been taken from the Revenue Records as
survey figures are not available by Tahsils. The totals of these figures,
therefore, do not agree with the figures of Imperial Table I which are based on
the survey data.
2. The difference, between some of the areas shown in this Table and those
given in the corresponding Table of 1921, is due to remeasurement or territorial
transfers. The details of transfers whether inter-district or provincial, which
affect the areas of Districts, are shown on the fly-leaf to Imperial Table I. Some
place during

changes have, however, taken

the decade

affecting certain Tahsils, as mentioned below :—

inside the Districts,

a. In the Sialkot District, Zafarwal and Raya have ceased to be Tahsils,
while a new Tahsil called Narowal has been formed.
b. In the Sheikhupura District, three new Tahsils, Sheikhupura,
Shahdara and Nankana Sahib, have been formed out of the

Khangah Dogran

and Sharalpur Tahsils, and the area transferred from the Lyallpur District.

e. Inthe Muzaffargarh District, Sanawan Tahsil has been abolished,
while a new Tahsil called Kot Adu has been formed.
d. In Mandi State, Harbhag Tahsi! has been abolished, and another

called Jogindernagar has been formed.
e. In Suket State, portions have been taken out of Bhal and
Tahsils to form the new Dehar Tahsil.
.

Karsog

In Kapurthala State, the Bastiat Tahsil has ceased to exist, having been

amalgamated with the Kapurthala Tahsil.
gq, In Fridkot State, Kot Kapura Tahsil has ceased to exist.
h. In Nabha State, the Tahsil of that name has been added to the Amloh

while Jaitu and Dbanaula Tahsils have been added to the Phul Tahail

Tahsil:

of the same State.
i. In Bahawalpur State, Naushehra Tahsil has given place to one named
,
Rahim Yar Khan.
The changes that have altered the area of certain Tahsils are given
below :—
:

PROVINCIAL
POPULATION

TABLE

OF DISTRICTS AND TAHSILS
LITERACY.

Il.

BY RELIGION AND

1. The figures by religion for each Tahsil have been taken from the

Village Tables.

2. The figures for literacy by age have been taken from Table XIII,
3. The figures of Hindus have been split up into “ Brahmans, ”
‘Other Hindus * and “ Depressed Classes, ” which for the purposes of this
Table, are Chamar, Chuira, Dagi & Koli, and Sansi, The figures for
Ad-Dharmis have been shown separately in columns 14—17, and in order to
obtain the total for all Depressed Classes these should be added to
columns 10—13,
